##
xplorertech00.wordpress.com@xplorertech00.wordpress.com at 2026-10-26T05:58:03.000Z ##

The memory limit was supposed to stop the request. It became the exploit

[🖼 xplorertech00.wordpress.com/wp…]

PHP2Shell, as a vulnerability, an impact, a public technical chain, and a patch that is not finished until the worker restarts.

26 October 2026 · Defensive reading of Calif, Wiz, and OpenAI · Not an exploit write-up

PHP has treated memory_limit as a safety rail for two decades. Hit the ceiling, abort the request, move on. For PHP2Shell, that story is backwards.

The fatal is not the recovery. The fatal is the primitive. A safety check fires while the engine has already published request state and has not committed it. Teardown trusts the publish step. On the SAPIs that keep the worker, the next request trusts the leftovers.

That is the whole post. The rest is the vulnerability, what it costs, what the public research actually demonstrated, and what “patched” means in a process that is already running.

The vulnerability

[🖼 xplorertech00.wordpress.com/wp…]

PHP2Shell is two bugs in the PHP engine, not in an application.

CVE-2026-109618CVE-2026-109619ShapeOut-of-memory corruptionStale global stateScoreCVSS v4.0 9.2CVSS v4.0 9.2WhenRequest setup, before the scriptA later request on a worker that survivedFixed21 October 202621 October 2026

Both are reached while PHP is still parsing the request. No login. No bug in WordPress, Drupal, Laravel, or the one-file script you deployed last year. The guest application has not started.

The affected range runs from PHP 5.4 through the unpatched 8.x releases. Web SAPIs are in scope: Apache mod_php, PHP-FPM, php-cgi, FrankenPHP, LiteSpeed. The PHP project and the researchers agree the practical risk is highest on mod_php, because that worker shuts the failed request down and then stays alive.

Two engine rules make it possible.

Publish before commit. Some paths mark a structure as live, then do the allocation that fills it in. If the second step fatals, teardown still believes the first step.

Abort is not rollback. memory_limit is a non-local exit (zend_bailout), not an unwind of every mutation that already happened. Shutdown walks whatever the counters say is live.

This shape is old. In 2004 Stefan Esser showed that a memory-limit fatal could interrupt PHP where interruption was unsafe. A 2020 report, PHP bug #78876, and a 2024 audit both touched the out-of-memory behavior. Exploitation was not proven, so it stayed a cleanup glitch. Calif, in OpenAI’s Patch the Planet work, and Wiz, independently, showed the chain holds under default settings. Frontier models cut the cost of a chain that had sat near the edge of manual research for years. That is a claim about discovery cost. It is not a claim that every host falls over on the first packet.

The impact

[🖼 xplorertech00.wordpress.com/wp…]

Successful use is unauthenticated remote code execution as the PHP worker, or a read of that worker’s memory. Wiz describes the second outcome as Heartbleed-shaped: passwords, tokens, and keys that belong to other requests, because the worker is shared. Its heap is not a per-customer enclave.

Calif reports pre-auth confirmation on the builds they tested:

  • WordPress, Drupal, Joomla, MediaWiki
  • Magento, Laravel, Symfony
  • Roundcube, phpMyAdmin, Nextcloud, WHMCS
  • some arbitrary scripts, not only full applications

Those are their results, on their builds. “Default config, before the script runs” is a claim to check in a lab you own. It is not a census of the internet.

What follows from the architecture, without inventing a victim count:

PHP 8.1 and older have no upstream release. Those hosts stay vulnerable until someone backports or the stack moves.

Patching the CMS does not move the bug. The interpreter fails first.

mod_php and PHP-FPM are not the same risk. A startup fatal that becomes the next request’s state on mod_php is often just a dead worker on FPM. FPM does not remove the post-startup path.

A leaked advisory beat the embargo. Scanning around 17–21 October 2026 shows interest. Interest is not proof that every probe achieved code execution. Reliable chains depend on SAPI, build, and whether session.upload_progress is enabled. That setting is a common default, not a universal one.

The public chain, as a technical write-up
This is a reading of Calif’s published mechanism. It is not a reproduction, and it is not a procedure you should run against anything you do not own.

Calif’s analysis is about request startup.

PHP registers $_POST and $_FILES inside php_request_startup(), before the target file runs. Nested form names become HashTables. An empty RFC1867 upload part (filename=””) still creates the six $_FILES slots, and a deeply nested name makes PHP build that tree for each slot. The body can stay modest while the allocator works hard. That is how a request reaches memory_limit without first dying on post_max_size. Chunked HTTP/1.1 or HTTP/2 widens the gap: declared length and parsed bytes are not the same check.

The line that matters is the order inside _zend_hash_str_add_or_update_i:

C

 idx = ht->nNumUsed++;  ht->nNumOfElements++;   p = ht->arData + idx;  p->key = zend_string_init(str, len, persistent); /* may zend_bailout() */

The live count moves first. The key allocation can bail out. Teardown walks nNumUsed and treats the slot as a real bucket. The key was never written. The bucket is whatever bytes were already there.

That sequence is CVE-2026-109618. Increment the count, the bucket becomes visible to teardown, the key allocation fatals, initialization is skipped, teardown trusts the count.

The same ordering is the second bug, in session upload progress.

php_hash_environment() parses the multipart body before zend_activate_modules(). Session upload progress may open a session in that window, while PG(modules_activated) is still 0. If parsing fatals, normal module shutdown can be skipped, the request heap is still freed, and the worker does not necessarily die.

On Apache mod_php, cleanup runs even when startup failed, then the process is reused. A later request can initialize the session against PS(id) and PS(http_session_vars) pointing into the previous request’s freed heap. Request A stores request-owned pointers, fatals, skips session shutdown, frees the heap, and the worker survives. Request B reads the leftovers. That stale global state is CVE-2026-109619.

On PHP-FPM, a startup failure takes FPM_EXIT_SOFTWARE. The half-built table dies with the worker. The second bug still exists there, but it needs a fatal after startup so the process is still around to be wrong.

Calif’s useful generalization is the pattern, not a payload: publish or destroy state, do fallible work, then commit. A non-local fatal during the fallible step skips the commit. A later consumer trusts the false state. They used that pattern to move from the HashTable site to session upload progress. One ordering bug, a family of call sites.

Public repositories showed up within hours of the leak. Many are lab-only: a specific official image, display_errors, mod_php prefork, offsets for that build. An offset is a property of a binary. It is not a property of the CVE. Treat GitHub stars as a measure of attention.

The patch

The upstream fixes shipped on 21 October 2026:

BranchFirst fixed release8.58.5.128.48.4.278.38.3.368.28.2.35

Confirm the strings on php.net before you trust a mirror, a container tag, or this page. PHP 8.1 and below are end of life. A distro backport counts only when that vendor’s advisory names these two CVEs.

A patch here means the engine no longer publishes the bucket, or the session global, before the fallible allocation, and no longer lets a bailout leave that object for teardown or for the next request. It does not mean a WAF rule, a disabled ini setting, or an updated WordPress.

Installing the package updates a file on disk. The worker keeps the mapping it already loaded. Until Apache children or FPM workers are recycled, the host is patched and still running the bug.

Disabling session.upload_progress, or changing memory_limit, changes which paths are easy. It does not restore the rollback. The update plus the restart is the fix.

The only lab this post asks for is the one that checks the patch. It does not attack anything.

Bash

 # The CLI binary is not the web SAPI. Check both.  php -v  php-fpm -v 2>/dev/null || true

One script, requested once, then deleted:

PHP

 <?php  header('Content-Type: text/plain');  echo PHP_VERSION, "\n", PHP_SAPI, "\n";

You want a fixed version and the SAPI you expected: fpm-fcgi, apache2handler, litespeed, or frankenphp. Restart the pool or Apache and request it again. If the version does not change, you restarted the wrong service, or the vhost points at another binary. On Debian and Ubuntu, php -v often prints a distro string that is not upstream 8.3.36. Read the package changelog.

For hunting, look for leads. Reporting around 17–21 October 2026 describes multipart POSTs tied to worker crashes and odd session-upload behavior before the advisory. Fatal memory errors on requests that never reached a route you recognize, then worker recycling, are a lead. They are not proof of compromise.

The fatal is part of the transaction

We teach operators that resource limits fail safe. PHP’s allocator fails closed on the next emalloc. The request machinery around it does not. It publishes a bucket, a session id, a global, and only then does the work that is allowed to abort.

That is the vulnerability: an incomplete transaction in request setup. The impact is code execution or a read of a shared worker, before the application runs, on a runtime that goes back to PHP 5.4. The public chain is that ordering, demonstrated by Calif and Wiz on stacks they named, and not a universal one-click. The patch is a new binary in the worker’s address space, not a new package sitting on disk.

Print PHP_VERSION and PHP_SAPI from the process that faces the internet. Match them to a fixed release. Restart that process. Print them again. Anything short of that is a plan to patch, not a patch.

Calif — PHP2Shell

Wiz Research — PHP2Shell

OpenAI — coordinated disclosure

##
TrustedAlpaca at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.872Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

##
TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T16:37:24.000Z ##

This week gave me Heartbleed vibes again, a vulnerability that haunted everyone for years. PHP powers 70% to 80% of the internet's websites, including widely popular CMS platforms like WordPress and Drupal. Critical PHP RCE (Remote Code Execution) vulnerabilities, registered under CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 and dubbed "PHP2Shell", were leaked online before the official announcement for security patches took place. In fact, some threat actors attempted to exploit them up to four days before the PHP Foundation officially released patches for PHP versions 5.4 to 8.5.

What will make this situation worse in the coming months and years is the sheer number of legacy systems that cannot be updated. This happens for several reasons: the operating system or dependencies has reached its end of life, the software solution was discontinued and left unmanaged, or the sole person managing a fragile system left the company. Often, because a system still generates high profits, no one has dared to update it in years, leaving it hanging as low-hanging fruit for an easy compromise. Many organisations face a combination of these exact issues and many more.

Furthermore, typical patching cycles require a week or two of piloting to monitor for instabilities or system crashes. Companies are naturally reluctant to skip this testing phase, but the severity of this PHP vulnerability means standard patching cycles must be expedited immediately.

As AI makes waves across every IT and Cyber Security sector, the threat landscape is shifting massively. We are now seeing threat actors use AI to reverse engineer solutions and compromise systems before security patches are even available. Years ago, before AI became a mainstream trend, this level of speed would have been unimaginable. Yet, as with every technological advancement and revolution, here we are.

#php #cybersecurity #patchmanagement #php2shell #wordpress

##
beyondmachines1@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T12:01:42.000Z ##

PHP2Shell: What Is Easy and What Is Hard to Exploit

PHP2Shell (CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619, CVSS 9.2) is a pair of pre-authentication RCE flaws in PHP's request parser, reachable through any PHP endpoint on default configs (session upload progress and file uploads). Crashing a worker is trivial but reliable code execution needs per-build memory offsets. The problem is that one calibrated exploit works on every identical stock image like default docker images.

**If your website or application runs on PHP, update right now to PHP 8.2.35, 8.3.36, 8.4.27 or 8.5.12 and restart all PHP and web server workers, because a patched server with old workers still running is still vulnerable. If you can't patch immediately, set `session.upload_progress.enabled = Off` as a temporary stopgap (it blocks only one of the two attack paths), restart all workers, and watch your logs for memory errors, crashes and web processes starting shells, since a successful attack can look like a perfectly normal request.**
#cybersecurity #infosec #knowledge #awareness
https://beyondmachines.net/event_details/obstacles-to-weaponizing-php2shell-likely-constrain-immediate-widespread-exploitation-0-3-u-e-l/gD2P6Ple2L

PHP has various global state that temporarily points into request memory. In some scenarios, global state may not be correctly reset, or may be repopulated after being reset. The global state may then refer to request memory even after it has been freed. Subsequent requests may read or branch on this stale state before it is fully initialized. ##
xplorertech00.wordpress.com@xplorertech00.wordpress.com at 2026-10-26T05:58:03.000Z ##

The memory limit was supposed to stop the request. It became the exploit

[🖼 xplorertech00.wordpress.com/wp…]

PHP2Shell, as a vulnerability, an impact, a public technical chain, and a patch that is not finished until the worker restarts.

26 October 2026 · Defensive reading of Calif, Wiz, and OpenAI · Not an exploit write-up

PHP has treated memory_limit as a safety rail for two decades. Hit the ceiling, abort the request, move on. For PHP2Shell, that story is backwards.

The fatal is not the recovery. The fatal is the primitive. A safety check fires while the engine has already published request state and has not committed it. Teardown trusts the publish step. On the SAPIs that keep the worker, the next request trusts the leftovers.

That is the whole post. The rest is the vulnerability, what it costs, what the public research actually demonstrated, and what “patched” means in a process that is already running.

The vulnerability

[🖼 xplorertech00.wordpress.com/wp…]

PHP2Shell is two bugs in the PHP engine, not in an application.

CVE-2026-109618CVE-2026-109619ShapeOut-of-memory corruptionStale global stateScoreCVSS v4.0 9.2CVSS v4.0 9.2WhenRequest setup, before the scriptA later request on a worker that survivedFixed21 October 202621 October 2026

Both are reached while PHP is still parsing the request. No login. No bug in WordPress, Drupal, Laravel, or the one-file script you deployed last year. The guest application has not started.

The affected range runs from PHP 5.4 through the unpatched 8.x releases. Web SAPIs are in scope: Apache mod_php, PHP-FPM, php-cgi, FrankenPHP, LiteSpeed. The PHP project and the researchers agree the practical risk is highest on mod_php, because that worker shuts the failed request down and then stays alive.

Two engine rules make it possible.

Publish before commit. Some paths mark a structure as live, then do the allocation that fills it in. If the second step fatals, teardown still believes the first step.

Abort is not rollback. memory_limit is a non-local exit (zend_bailout), not an unwind of every mutation that already happened. Shutdown walks whatever the counters say is live.

This shape is old. In 2004 Stefan Esser showed that a memory-limit fatal could interrupt PHP where interruption was unsafe. A 2020 report, PHP bug #78876, and a 2024 audit both touched the out-of-memory behavior. Exploitation was not proven, so it stayed a cleanup glitch. Calif, in OpenAI’s Patch the Planet work, and Wiz, independently, showed the chain holds under default settings. Frontier models cut the cost of a chain that had sat near the edge of manual research for years. That is a claim about discovery cost. It is not a claim that every host falls over on the first packet.

The impact

[🖼 xplorertech00.wordpress.com/wp…]

Successful use is unauthenticated remote code execution as the PHP worker, or a read of that worker’s memory. Wiz describes the second outcome as Heartbleed-shaped: passwords, tokens, and keys that belong to other requests, because the worker is shared. Its heap is not a per-customer enclave.

Calif reports pre-auth confirmation on the builds they tested:

  • WordPress, Drupal, Joomla, MediaWiki
  • Magento, Laravel, Symfony
  • Roundcube, phpMyAdmin, Nextcloud, WHMCS
  • some arbitrary scripts, not only full applications

Those are their results, on their builds. “Default config, before the script runs” is a claim to check in a lab you own. It is not a census of the internet.

What follows from the architecture, without inventing a victim count:

PHP 8.1 and older have no upstream release. Those hosts stay vulnerable until someone backports or the stack moves.

Patching the CMS does not move the bug. The interpreter fails first.

mod_php and PHP-FPM are not the same risk. A startup fatal that becomes the next request’s state on mod_php is often just a dead worker on FPM. FPM does not remove the post-startup path.

A leaked advisory beat the embargo. Scanning around 17–21 October 2026 shows interest. Interest is not proof that every probe achieved code execution. Reliable chains depend on SAPI, build, and whether session.upload_progress is enabled. That setting is a common default, not a universal one.

The public chain, as a technical write-up
This is a reading of Calif’s published mechanism. It is not a reproduction, and it is not a procedure you should run against anything you do not own.

Calif’s analysis is about request startup.

PHP registers $_POST and $_FILES inside php_request_startup(), before the target file runs. Nested form names become HashTables. An empty RFC1867 upload part (filename=””) still creates the six $_FILES slots, and a deeply nested name makes PHP build that tree for each slot. The body can stay modest while the allocator works hard. That is how a request reaches memory_limit without first dying on post_max_size. Chunked HTTP/1.1 or HTTP/2 widens the gap: declared length and parsed bytes are not the same check.

The line that matters is the order inside _zend_hash_str_add_or_update_i:

C

 idx = ht->nNumUsed++;  ht->nNumOfElements++;   p = ht->arData + idx;  p->key = zend_string_init(str, len, persistent); /* may zend_bailout() */

The live count moves first. The key allocation can bail out. Teardown walks nNumUsed and treats the slot as a real bucket. The key was never written. The bucket is whatever bytes were already there.

That sequence is CVE-2026-109618. Increment the count, the bucket becomes visible to teardown, the key allocation fatals, initialization is skipped, teardown trusts the count.

The same ordering is the second bug, in session upload progress.

php_hash_environment() parses the multipart body before zend_activate_modules(). Session upload progress may open a session in that window, while PG(modules_activated) is still 0. If parsing fatals, normal module shutdown can be skipped, the request heap is still freed, and the worker does not necessarily die.

On Apache mod_php, cleanup runs even when startup failed, then the process is reused. A later request can initialize the session against PS(id) and PS(http_session_vars) pointing into the previous request’s freed heap. Request A stores request-owned pointers, fatals, skips session shutdown, frees the heap, and the worker survives. Request B reads the leftovers. That stale global state is CVE-2026-109619.

On PHP-FPM, a startup failure takes FPM_EXIT_SOFTWARE. The half-built table dies with the worker. The second bug still exists there, but it needs a fatal after startup so the process is still around to be wrong.

Calif’s useful generalization is the pattern, not a payload: publish or destroy state, do fallible work, then commit. A non-local fatal during the fallible step skips the commit. A later consumer trusts the false state. They used that pattern to move from the HashTable site to session upload progress. One ordering bug, a family of call sites.

Public repositories showed up within hours of the leak. Many are lab-only: a specific official image, display_errors, mod_php prefork, offsets for that build. An offset is a property of a binary. It is not a property of the CVE. Treat GitHub stars as a measure of attention.

The patch

The upstream fixes shipped on 21 October 2026:

BranchFirst fixed release8.58.5.128.48.4.278.38.3.368.28.2.35

Confirm the strings on php.net before you trust a mirror, a container tag, or this page. PHP 8.1 and below are end of life. A distro backport counts only when that vendor’s advisory names these two CVEs.

A patch here means the engine no longer publishes the bucket, or the session global, before the fallible allocation, and no longer lets a bailout leave that object for teardown or for the next request. It does not mean a WAF rule, a disabled ini setting, or an updated WordPress.

Installing the package updates a file on disk. The worker keeps the mapping it already loaded. Until Apache children or FPM workers are recycled, the host is patched and still running the bug.

Disabling session.upload_progress, or changing memory_limit, changes which paths are easy. It does not restore the rollback. The update plus the restart is the fix.

The only lab this post asks for is the one that checks the patch. It does not attack anything.

Bash

 # The CLI binary is not the web SAPI. Check both.  php -v  php-fpm -v 2>/dev/null || true

One script, requested once, then deleted:

PHP

 <?php  header('Content-Type: text/plain');  echo PHP_VERSION, "\n", PHP_SAPI, "\n";

You want a fixed version and the SAPI you expected: fpm-fcgi, apache2handler, litespeed, or frankenphp. Restart the pool or Apache and request it again. If the version does not change, you restarted the wrong service, or the vhost points at another binary. On Debian and Ubuntu, php -v often prints a distro string that is not upstream 8.3.36. Read the package changelog.

For hunting, look for leads. Reporting around 17–21 October 2026 describes multipart POSTs tied to worker crashes and odd session-upload behavior before the advisory. Fatal memory errors on requests that never reached a route you recognize, then worker recycling, are a lead. They are not proof of compromise.

The fatal is part of the transaction

We teach operators that resource limits fail safe. PHP’s allocator fails closed on the next emalloc. The request machinery around it does not. It publishes a bucket, a session id, a global, and only then does the work that is allowed to abort.

That is the vulnerability: an incomplete transaction in request setup. The impact is code execution or a read of a shared worker, before the application runs, on a runtime that goes back to PHP 5.4. The public chain is that ordering, demonstrated by Calif and Wiz on stacks they named, and not a universal one-click. The patch is a new binary in the worker’s address space, not a new package sitting on disk.

Print PHP_VERSION and PHP_SAPI from the process that faces the internet. Match them to a fixed release. Restart that process. Print them again. Anything short of that is a plan to patch, not a patch.

🤖 AI generated summary

##
TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T16:37:24.000Z ##

This week gave me Heartbleed vibes again, a vulnerability that haunted everyone for years. PHP powers 70% to 80% of the internet's websites, including widely popular CMS platforms like WordPress and Drupal. Critical PHP RCE (Remote Code Execution) vulnerabilities, registered under CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 and dubbed "PHP2Shell", were leaked online before the official announcement for security patches took place. In fact, some threat actors attempted to exploit them up to four days before the PHP Foundation officially released patches for PHP versions 5.4 to 8.5.

What will make this situation worse in the coming months and years is the sheer number of legacy systems that cannot be updated. This happens for several reasons: the operating system or dependencies has reached its end of life, the software solution was discontinued and left unmanaged, or the sole person managing a fragile system left the company. Often, because a system still generates high profits, no one has dared to update it in years, leaving it hanging as low-hanging fruit for an easy compromise. Many organisations face a combination of these exact issues and many more.

Furthermore, typical patching cycles require a week or two of piloting to monitor for instabilities or system crashes. Companies are naturally reluctant to skip this testing phase, but the severity of this PHP vulnerability means standard patching cycles must be expedited immediately.

As AI makes waves across every IT and Cyber Security sector, the threat landscape is shifting massively. We are now seeing threat actors use AI to reverse engineer solutions and compromise systems before security patches are even available. Years ago, before AI became a mainstream trend, this level of speed would have been unimaginable. Yet, as with every technological advancement and revolution, here we are.

#php #cybersecurity #patchmanagement #php2shell #wordpress

##
beyondmachines1@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T12:01:42.000Z ##

PHP2Shell: What Is Easy and What Is Hard to Exploit

PHP2Shell (CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619, CVSS 9.2) is a pair of pre-authentication RCE flaws in PHP's request parser, reachable through any PHP endpoint on default configs (session upload progress and file uploads). Crashing a worker is trivial but reliable code execution needs per-build memory offsets. The problem is that one calibrated exploit works on every identical stock image like default docker images.

**If your website or application runs on PHP, update right now to PHP 8.2.35, 8.3.36, 8.4.27 or 8.5.12 and restart all PHP and web server workers, because a patched server with old workers still running is still vulnerable. If you can't patch immediately, set `session.upload_progress.enabled = Off` as a temporary stopgap (it blocks only one of the two attack paths), restart all workers, and watch your logs for memory errors, crashes and web processes starting shells, since a successful attack can look like a perfectly normal request.**
#cybersecurity #infosec #knowledge #awareness
https://beyondmachines.net/event_details/obstacles-to-weaponizing-php2shell-likely-constrain-immediate-widespread-exploitation-0-3-u-e-l/gD2P6Ple2L

zeroplex@social.zeroplex.tw at 2026-10-24T04:19:26.000Z ##

PHP 漏洞通報是個失誤

原發文者承認，自己曾在 Hacker News 提前提及 PHP 即將修補的兩項可造成遠端程式碼執行（RCE, Remote Code Execution）的弱點，編號為 CVE（Common Vulnerabilities and Exposures，公開資安弱點編號）-2026-109618 與 CVE-2026-109619。他原本只是想提醒同樣自行編譯 PHP 的人儘快留意更新，卻意外干擾協調式弱點揭露（coordinated disclosure，讓修補與公開時程配合的做法），使既定公開時程調整，也增加許多人的應變負擔，因此公開致歉並表示願意承擔責任。

他說，發文當下完全不知道存在保密期限（embargo）；他的消息來源告知沒有保密約定，而對方也是從更上游得到相同說法。這起事件讓他深感不安、睡眠受影響，並認為自己的善意提醒造成了意料之外的連鎖效應。他也表示，日後會更謹慎處理未公開的資安訊息。
https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50193416

##
TrustedAlpaca at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.872Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

##
MissConstrue@mefi.social at 2026-10-26T03:21:11.000Z ##

Oh man, light a candle for #infosec, #sysop, and #ops, cause if you thought the #php #cve was fun, wait till you hear about CVE-2026-111497, AKA as Node2Shell, HTTP2Shell, and Broccoli2Shell, a high-severity out-of-bounds write vulnerability in the Brotli decoder that can permit remote code execution (#RCE). The bug allows data to overwrite memory buffers when a process decompresses untrusted Brotli streams. It was surfaced by #OpenAI’s red team.

Fixed versions were released this weekend, but systems using system-linked Brotli libraries (e.g., Debian, Alpine) must verify their distribution-specific package updates, as the Node.js binary release does not patch those external dependencies.

While researchers from Imperva demonstrated successful code execution on a specific Node.js 24.22.0 Linux arm64 build, no widespread exploitation in the wild has been reported as of October 25, 2026..

https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2026-111497

blobster@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T09:15:58.000Z ##

@jschauma For Debian sid/unstable, there is a patched package in the Bug Tracking System by Salvatore Bonaccorso who is part of the Debian security team; his changes are also in the salsa Git repo).

The bug log seems to indicate that the Debian maintainer doesn't consider there is any hurry for sid. So I downloaded the source package, visually inspected the patch, applied it, verified that the two modified source files were now identical to those from upstream commit b418fa81c48262a86a9 which is referenced at the bottom of the Debian security tracker page for CVE-2026-111497, built the source package and upgraded the relevant binary packages.

To be on the safe side, I also uninstalled libio-compress-brotli-perl which is built from a different source package, although that one is quite possibly not affected by the bug (the package was only recommended by other packages I have installed, not depended on).

##
beyondmachines1@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-24T10:01:43.000Z ##

Node.js Releases Critical Security Updates to Fix Brotli Remote Code Execution and Network Flaws

Node.js released security updates for 10 vulnerabilities, including a critical remote code execution flaw in the Brotli decoder (CVE-2026-111497) and multiple network validation bypasses. These vulnerabilities reside in the core runtime, requiring administrators to update host systems, rebuild container images, and restart all services.

**If you run Node.js anywhere, update it right away to version 22.23.4, 24.22.1 or 26.12.1, then rebuild any containers or apps that bundle Node.js and restart all Node.js services (your usual dependency scanners won't catch this, so check the runtime versions yourself). If you're still on old versions like 20.x, 23.x or 25.x, they will never be fixed. Move to a supported version ASAP. Until you can patch, block Brotli-compressed (br) traffic at your proxy or firewall as a temporary shield.**
#cybersecurity #infosec #advisory #vulnerability
https://beyondmachines.net/event_details/node-js-releases-critical-security-updates-to-fix-brotli-remote-code-execution-and-network-flaws-e-l-q-t-c/gD2P6Ple2L

2/3 Also done today:

Checked the Node.js October security release (Brotli CVE-2026-111497). Our streaming server never decompresses untrusted Brotli data, so the risk is low. We'll update as soon as the FreeBSD package is out.

The streaming server now properly verifies the database's TLS certificate.

Rewrote our rc.d scripts the way rc.subr intends: supervisor pidfile, automatic restart after a crash, and "service restart" works on the first try. Background: https://blog.hofstede.it/freebsd-foundationals-rcd-the-service-framework-that-fits-in-your-head/

Package updates on the host and in all jails

nginx: separate cache directories, a nearby DNS resolver (thousands of DNS errors gone) and a catch-all for unknown hostnames

We retired our Tor onion service

Lots of small cleanups after a deep health check

##

CVE-2026-115557
(0 None)

EPSS: 0.53%

updated 2026-10-26T01:17:14.870000

1 posts

Python has added a denylist for known compromised certificates to the "ssl" module to ensure user safety even without certificate revocation configured. Known compromised certificates will automatically be rejected regardless of the SSLContext policy configuration.

TrustedAlpaca at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.872Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

#Gitlab #eMudhra #Node2Shell #CyberSecurity #Vulnerabilities

##

CVE-2026-115547
(9.1 CRITICAL)

EPSS: 0.50%

updated 2026-10-23T06:31:32

1 posts

An input validation issue in the Okta Access Gateway (OAG) Admin Console allows the processing of specially formatted configuration values. An authenticated administrator can leverage this to execute system commands within the context of the OAG service account.

beyondmachines1@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T15:01:42.000Z ##

Okta Patches Command Injection and Authentication Bypass in Access Gateway

Okta released security updates for Access Gateway to fix a command injection flaw (CVE-2026-115547) and an authentication bypass (CVE-2026-115546) that allow attackers to take full control of the appliance. The updates also address two remote code execution vulnerabilities in the PHP runtime used by the gateway.

**If you run Okta Access Gateway, upgrade it to version 2026.10.2 or later to fix the admin takeover flaws, and install version 2026.44.0 to fix the PHP flaws. Make sure the Admin Console can only be reached from trusted internal IP addresses, and check for any unexpected admin logins or configuration changes.**
#cybersecurity #infosec #advisory #vulnerability
https://beyondmachines.net/event_details/okta-patches-command-injection-and-authentication-bypass-in-access-gateway-r-q-6-8-m/gD2P6Ple2L

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

#Gitlab #eMudhra #Node2Shell #CyberSecurity #Vulnerabilities

CW: technikai sebezhetőség

Ruby 4.0.9, 3.4.12 és 3.3.13 javít három Onigmo regex-motort érintő hibát, amelyek négy kritikus GitLab RCE-hez vezettek (pl. CVE-2026-110264, CVE-2026-97229). Érintett: ha támadó-vezérelt sztringből fordítasz Regexp.new/union/compile vagy /#{input}/-et. Ideiglenes védelem: ne fordíts megbízhatatlan bemenetet; Regexp.timeout vagy mintahossz nem elég. Részletek: https://linuxmint.hu/hir/2026/10/harom-regexmotor-hibat-javitott-a-ruby-negy-kritikus-gitlab-rce-sebezhetoseget-okoztak

### Summary `PIL.EpsImagePlugin.Ghostscript()` builds the Ghostscript command line using the input file path (`fp.name`) **without sanitization**, placing it at `-f <infile>`. A filename beginning with `-` is interpreted by Ghostscript as an option, giving command-line argument injection; chainable to RCE (see Impact). Reproduces with Pillow's default invocation (with `-dSAFER` enabled). ### Deta

##
security_crawler_carl@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-24T12:57:17.000Z ##

Three critical flaws (CVE-2026-112900, CVE-2026-112393, CVE-2026-112403, CVSS 9.2 each) let a crafted EPS, TIFF, or JPEG file trigger remote code execution or heap corruption. The EPS one shells out to Ghostscript with your filename completely unguarded. That is not a bug in this tutorial. That is the mechanic.

To remove the debuff, upgrade Pillow to 13.0.0 immediately. (2/3)

The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

#Gitlab #eMudhra #Node2Shell #CyberSecurity #Vulnerabilities

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-24T20:32:38.000Z ##

🚨 PoC published for a critical unauthenticated Fortinet FortiSIEM remote code execution vulnerability; CVE-2026-106173

PoC: https://github.com/horizon3ai/CVE-2026-106173

The flaw affects FortiSIEM's gRPC QueryResultReq handler. Improper validation of attacker-controlled metadata allows path traversal and arbitrary file writes, which can be chained into remote code execution by overwriting files that are later executed by the system.

CVSS: 9.4 Critical
Affected: FortiSIEM 7.0.0 through 7.5.0 across multiple release branches
Fixed: FortiSIEM 7.5.1 or 7.6.0 and later

The PoC targets the exposed gRPC service on TCP/27918 and demonstrates blind command execution through the arbitrary file-write chain.

DarkWebInformer at 2026-10-26T00:29:02.832Z ##

🚨 KeePassXC 2.8.0 is out with several security fixes and a major move to Qt 6

Release: https://github.com/keepassxreboot/keepassxc/releases

https://keepassxc.org/download

The update fixes CVE-2026-69150, a use-after-free triggered when importing a crafted legacy KDB file, along with multiple additional browser integration, memory safety, and Windows security issues that are still awaiting CVE assignments.

Security fixes include:

• Crafted KDB import use-after-free
• Browser entry reference injection
• Out-of-bounds writes in native messaging
• Weak Pageant shared-memory mapping names on Windows
• Integer overflow and out-of-bounds read in the legacy KDB importer
• Improved clearing of decrypted database fragments from memory

KeePassXC 2.8.0 also adds remote database synchronization, configurable shortcuts, SSH key generation, Wayland Auto-Type support, Linux Quick Unlock, and native Windows Arm64 builds.

Neues zum PHP-Sicherheitsproblem

Aller guten Dinge sind drei. In PHP wurde eine dritte Sicherheitslücke (CVE-2026-115560) gefunden. Die wird aber kein Notfall-Update nach sich ziehen. Erstens ist sie schwierig ausnutzbar, und zweitens kann man sich mit der Konfigurationseinstellung “session.upload_progress.enabled=0" vor Angriffen schützen. Apropos schwierig ausnutzbar: Die beiden anderen Sicherheitslücken, die zusammengefasst als PHP2Shell bezeichnet werden, haben sich ebenfalls als schwierig ausnutzbar erwiesen. Zwar wurden schnell reichlich Angriffs-VERSUCHE beobachtet, aber bisher ist kein einziger Fall eines erfolgreichen Angriffs bekannt.
